# Dior Homme Intense — 2011 lineage / 2012 target

**Formula artifact state:** `PASS_EXACT_DECIMAL_SUCCESSOR`  
**Stock-lineage state:** `HOLD`  
**Successor formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-DHI12-v2`  
**Successor hash:** `d2b3206b72e749a611b87d110373c4ce92d74ef7dd27a3e01be70f3385c9808d`  
**Parent formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-DHI12-v1`  
**Parent hash:** `30aa985b4a2d430b165bb9d11b5549d35d7aa4a4899d50e538a230553b8e367c`

**Target identity:** Tailored lavender-cool iris moving into a pear-ambrette silk-talc halo, then dry cedar-vetiver and intimate musky warmth.

**Version scope:** A 2012 bottle or batch from the 2011-lineage Dior Homme Intense. Current Dior information supports iris, ambrette and Virginia cedar; the 2011/2012 lavender, pear and vetiver architecture is secondary historical evidence and must remain version-scoped.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Tailored Silk Iris  
**Primary motif:** A cool lavender-cut iris suit whose lining turns into pear-ambrette silk.  
**Secondary motif:** A cacao-coffee amber shadow that appears only after the iris has become tactile.  
**Tension:** Cosmetic softness versus masculine tailoring.  
**Release:** The powder is dried by cedar and vetiver; the fruit melts into musk rather than remaining a note.  
**Negative space:** Hedione, muguet air and restrained transparent wood keep the iris from becoming mauve paste.  
**Recurrence:** The pear impression vanishes after the opening, then returns as a fatty-fruity musk sheen in the late heart.  
**Texture progression:** cold aromatic cloth → silk talc → waxy iris → cocoa suede → dry cedar skin  
**Spatial identity:** Detailed and cool at close range; a smoother iris-musk aura at one arm’s length.  
**Late residue:** Dry cedar-vetiver, orris wax, soft tonka and a skin-bound metallic-clean musk whisper.  
**Controlled imperfection:** A hairline bitterness from petitgrain and a dark coffee-cocoa grain prevent cosmetic prettiness from becoming sterile.

| PEAR-SILK HALO | WAXY IRIS | texture_change | heart | Green-fruit and fatty musk cues turn powder into silk-talc and pear-liqueur texture. | Pear shampoo or lipstick sweetness. | 0.5×/1×/1.5× halo at fixed total load; repeated days. |
| WAXY IRIS | MUSK SKIN | rounding | heart_to_drydown | Multi-class musks extend wax into skin and fabric while softening sharp ionone edges. | Laundry fog or anosmia-dependent collapse. | Habanolide-led versus soft-macrocyclic ratio comparison. |
| AMBER-CACAO SHADOW | WAXY IRIS | texture_change | late_heart | Cocoa/coffee/coumarin add grain and shadow without becoming a food note. | Mocha dessert or dusty powder. | No-dark-trace versus full trace block at 2 and 8 h. |
| CEDAR-VETIVER FRAME | IRIS PETAL | dryness_change | heart_to_drydown | Dry woods masculinize and tailor the soft iris. | Pencil cedar, root mud or petal burial. | −20%/+20% frame with petal recognition endpoint. |
| FLORAL AIR | WAXY IRIS | diffusion_change | heart | Hedione/muguet create air around dense iris materials. | Shampoo floral blur. | Floral-air block ablation and Hedione ratio test. |
| PEAR-SILK HALO | AMBER-CACAO SHADOW | bridge | heart | The fruit disappears into warm amber, creating tactile continuity rather than a top-note jump. | Fruity gourmand drift. | Time-course descriptive profile 30 min to 4 h. |
| CEDAR-VETIVER FRAME | MUSK SKIN | persistence_change | drydown | Wood grain and musk split near-field detail from far-field aura. | Generic woody musk cloud. | Near/mid/far evaluation at 2 and 8 h. |
| LATE RESIDUE | TAILORED AROMATIC OPENING | uncertain | late_drydown | Hay, suede and orris may recall the opening's dry cloth texture after the lavender is gone. | Dry dusty residue with no recognizer. | 24 h residue comparison against no-refrain control. |

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** Full Tailored Silk Iris versus no pear-silk halo versus no cacao shadow versus exact ambrette comparator when acquired.

**Ablation blocks**

- TAILORED AROMATIC OPENING
- PEAR-SILK HALO
- AMBER-CACAO SHADOW
- CEDAR-VETIVER FRAME
- MUSK SKIN

**Perturbation controls**

- pear-silk halo ±20%
- waxy iris ±20%
- cedar-vetiver frame ±20%
- musk skin ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- lavender-to-iris transition remains continuous
- pear is tactile, not shampoo
- iris survives 8 h
- cacao/coffee remain non-gourmand
- positive liking and desire-to-smell-again on two days
- does not collapse into L’Homme or DHP

**Reference gate:** Authenticated 2012 reference required for version-specific similarity.

**Temporal question:** Does lavender/pear-ambrette dissolve into iris without a cosmetic-to-wood discontinuity?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

# Dior Homme Parfum — 2025

**Formula artifact state:** `PASS_EXACT_DECIMAL_SUCCESSOR`  
**Stock-lineage state:** `HOLD`  
**Successor formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-DHP25-v2`  
**Successor hash:** `f69cb4e5133bbe12d280ca9c075fb828ac8637fc2aba60f613731b9c90aa4646`  
**Parent formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-DHP25-v1`  
**Parent hash:** `bfd507ceab9636f80c35c3ded77fe3f70d2f78c99c6e561a25bc49d824dc3187`

**Target identity:** A complete iris from aerial petal to dark rhizome, held against a firm patchouli-vetiver-coumarin amberwood base.

**Version scope:** Current Francis Kurkdjian edition launched in 2025.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Petal Under Pressure  
**Primary motif:** A pale iris petal suspended above a black-violet rhizome.  
**Secondary motif:** Cut hay, vetiver and patchouli form a dry field compressed by a polished amberwood grip.  
**Tension:** Powdery caress versus ambery pressure.  
**Release:** The base does not simply crush the petal; it gradually turns petal into wax, wax into root, and root into polished dark wood.  
**Negative space:** Hedione, muguet air, magnolia and floral transmission leave a lit chamber above the dense base.  
**Recurrence:** The petal disappears under patchouli at two hours, then reappears as a violet wax contour around the eight-hour woods.  
**Texture progression:** cold petal air → wax bloom → dark rhizome → cut hay → polished mineral wood  
**Spatial identity:** The far field is firm and woody; the near field retains floral wax and root detail.  
**Late residue:** Iris-root wax, vetiver, patchouli, hay and three-way amberwood pressure with a suede edge.  
**Controlled imperfection:** A trace of root earth, roasted grain and saffron-suede gives the immaculate structure a pulse.

| IRIS PETAL AIR | RHIZOME DENSITY | bridge | heart | The two iris poles form one complete flower-root object. | Stacked floral and muddy root modules. | Petal-only, root-only, and 20/40/60% ratio ladder. |
| RHIZOME DENSITY | CUT-HAY COUMARIN | texture_change | heart_to_drydown | Hay and coumarin dry the wax and amplify coffee/chocolate associations without food literalness. | Dusty attic, carrot earth or cocoa dessert. | Hay/coumarin ablation and ±20% test. |
| PATCHOULI DENSITY | RHIZOME DENSITY | enhancement | heart_to_drydown | Patchouli deepens root darkness and connects iris to earth. | Mud or camphor swallowing petal. | Patchouli 0.7×/1×/1.3× with petal survival endpoint. |
| VETIVER TENSION | IRIS PETAL AIR | suppression | heart_to_drydown | Vetiver supplies dry tensile contrast but may suppress the tender petal. | Smoky root and petal loss. | Matched −20%/+20% vetiver test at 30 min, 2 h, 8 h. |
| AMBERWOOD GRIP | IRIS PETAL AIR | collision_risk | drydown | Distributed amberwoods create firm projection while allowing a wax contour to survive. | Scratchy monolith or anosmic wall. | Three-way amberwood ratio perturbation; blind preference and petal recognition. |
| MUSK POLISH | AMBERWOOD GRIP | rounding | drydown | Soft clean musks polish pressure edges without becoming a laundry chassis. | Laundry flattening and loss of root grain. | No-musk and low/high musk controls. |
| IRIS PETAL AIR | AMBERWOOD GRIP | persistence_change | heart_to_late | The desired recurrence is petal transformed into a wax ring around late woods. | Petal fully extinguished by 2 h. | Time-course 5 min to 24 h with unified-iris score. |
| RHIZOME DENSITY | MUSK POLISH | texture_change | late_drydown | Musk may convert root wax into skin-like mineral suede. | Cosmetic paste. | 8 h and 24 h residue triangle/paired comparison. |

| Natural dual-iris granularity | `WEAK` | Iris pallida / orris absolute or concrete comparator with lot documentation | `MISSING` | **HIGH_VALUE ARCHITECTURAL EXPANSION** | The current irone, ionone and product-basis iris system can model functions, but not establish the complete natural petal/rhizome texture or lot-specific minor constituents. | The iris may read polished and abstract rather than simultaneously tender, rooty, coffee-dark and floral. | Root mud, carrot, fatty wax or cosmetic lipstick can overwhelm petal air. | Distributed synthetic iris system versus the same system plus natural comparator, with root and petal sub-block ablations at equal total load. |
| Petal survival under dense Parfum base | `EVIDENCE_GAP` | Measured headspace and coded temporal sensory data | `NOT TESTED` | **EVIDENCE GAP, NOT INVENTORY GAP** | A computational release model cannot prove that tender iris remains perceptible over patchouli, vetiver, coumarin and amberwoods. | The build can become a dark woody-rhizome monolith. | Not a purchase overdose; high base load can suppress petal detail. | Full build versus −20% amberwood, −20% patchouli and +20% petal-air blocks; matched concentration, coded 5 min to 24 h testing. |

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** Full Petal Under Pressure versus petal-only, rhizome-only, no-pressure, and −20% pressure-base controls.

**Ablation blocks**

- PETAL IGNITION
- IRIS PETAL AIR
- RHIZOME DENSITY
- CUT-HAY COUMARIN
- PATCHOULI DENSITY
- VETIVER TENSION
- AMBERWOOD GRIP

**Perturbation controls**

- petal air ±20%
- rhizome density ±20%
- patchouli ±20%
- vetiver ±20%
- amberwood grip ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- petal recognizable through 2 h
- unified iris stronger than either pole alone
- rhizome is dark without mud/carrot
- hay dry without attic dust
- amberwood firm without scratch
- positive repeated-day liking
- distinct from DHI

**Reference gate:** Current 2025 reference bottle required for similarity.

**Temporal question:** Does tender petal remain legible while rhizome and ambery grip intensify?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

## Authority boundary

Source admission, package installation, formula authority, inventory mutation, stock authority, bottle authority, physical execution, sensory and analytical authority, measured headspace, strict empirical OAV, safety, procurement, compounding, repository installation, publication and release remain false.

**Target identity:** Sparkling mandarin and open-air freshness crossing a clean cedar spine into almond-tonka and skin-like white musk.

**Version scope:** Current Eau de Toilette architecture.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Mandarin Air / Cedar Skin  
**Primary motif:** A juicy mandarin arc thrown through open air onto clean cedar.  
**Secondary motif:** Almond-tonka warmth and a radiant white-musk skin trail.  
**Tension:** Outdoor kinetic freshness versus bare-skin sensuality.  
**Release:** The air collapses inward into cedar, then cedar softens into tonka and skin musk.  
**Negative space:** Marine materials stay below the point of blue-fragrance identity; floral-air materials carve space between citrus and cedar.  
**Recurrence:** Mandarin returns in the drydown as a warm peel echo inside tonka and white musk.  
**Texture progression:** sparkling peel → wind on skin → clean cedar → almond cream → luminous musk  
**Spatial identity:** Bright kinetic opening in the far field, with softer cedar-tonka detail close to skin.  
**Late residue:** Dry cedar, almond-tonka, sandalwood fibers and a clean metallic-soft musk blend.  
**Controlled imperfection:** A green-bitter petitgrain seam keeps the sporty cleanliness from becoming shower-gel polish.

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** Full Mandarin Air / Cedar Skin versus no-air bridge, low/high musk geometry, and exact Italian mandarin comparator.

**Ablation blocks**

- MANDARIN PROPULSION
- OPEN-AIR FRESHNESS
- GREEN KINETIC SEAM
- CEDAR SPINE
- TONKA ALMOND
- WHITE-MUSK SKIN

**Perturbation controls**

- air field ±20%
- cedar spine ±20%
- tonka almond ±20%
- musk skin ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- mandarin reads succulent and kinetic
- no aquatic melon or blue shower gel
- cedar remains clean and textured
- tonka stays almond-soft not syrupy
- white musk envelops without laundry fog
- positive liking on two days
- distinct from Eau Extrême

**Reference gate:** Verified current EDT bottle and exact mandarin lot required for target-fidelity claim.

**Temporal question:** Does mandarin memory remain as cedar-tonka-musk takes over?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

# Chanel Allure Homme Édition Blanche Eau de Parfum

**Formula artifact state:** `PASS_EXACT_DECIMAL_SUCCESSOR`  
**Stock-lineage state:** `HOLD`  
**Successor formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-EB-v2`  
**Successor hash:** `436ccfff9b8cbc70ee1587cd8b12eec9917d0a830706578d7c91a31da3716f16`  
**Parent formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-EB-v1`  
**Parent hash:** `097103e8459c9c893c65571181ec5066ac9f56b9e93428c1216d5d6b472cdd7d`

**Target identity:** A cold, forceful lemon flash flowing into warm sandalwood cream, dry vetiver and restrained vanilla.

**Version scope:** Current Eau de Parfum architecture.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Lemon White Heat  
**Primary motif:** A blade of cold bitter lemon cutting through warm sandalwood cream.  
**Secondary motif:** A dry Haitian-style vetiver seam and restrained Madagascar-style vanilla glow.  
**Tension:** Cold acidic brightness versus warm creamy depth.  
**Release:** The cold lemon does not disappear; it becomes a pale bitter line running through the warm woods.  
**Negative space:** Ozonic air, muguet transmission and vetiver dryness prevent the sandalwood/vanilla block from becoming custard.  
**Recurrence:** A lemon rind echo reappears at two hours when vetiver exposes the citrus bitterness inside the cream.  
**Texture progression:** frosted lemon peel → mineral air → dry vetiver thread → sandalwood milk → vanilla skin  
**Spatial identity:** Cold brightness projects; warm sandalwood and vanilla stay closer and bloom with body heat.  
**Late residue:** Creamy fibrous sandalwood, dry vetiver, pale vanilla, incense and soft musk.  
**Controlled imperfection:** A pithy bitterness and a thread of root dryness keep the white cream from becoming edible.

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** Full Lemon White Heat versus no-cold-air, no-vetiver-seam, low/high vanilla, and exact-origin natural comparators.

**Ablation blocks**

- LEMON ENERGY
- COLD AIR
- BITTER PEEL
- VETIVER SEAM
- SANDALWOOD CREAM
- VANILLA WARMTH

**Perturbation controls**

- lemon energy ±20%
- cold air ±20%
- vetiver seam ±20%
- sandalwood cream ±20%
- vanilla warmth ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- cold lemon and warm cream are simultaneous
- lemon persists as bitter line
- no cleaner or lemon pie
- sandalwood creamy but fibrous
- vetiver dry, not dirty
- positive liking and second-smell desire on two days
- distinct from AHS and Eau Extrême

**Reference gate:** Current EDP reference plus exact-origin natural lots for full target-fidelity.

**Temporal question:** Does lemon transform into warm cream without becoming lemon pie or disappearing abruptly?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

**Target identity:** Mandarin adrenaline over green coniferous cypress, wrapped in dense white musk and almond-tonka warmth.

**Version scope:** Current Eau de Parfum architecture.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Cypress Pulse  
**Primary motif:** A mandarin spark hitting a green cypress spine.  
**Secondary motif:** A dense almond-tonka core inside a persistent white-musk field.  
**Tension:** Cold green propulsion versus enveloping musky warmth.  
**Release:** The coniferous pulse slows and folds inward, becoming a warm musk-tonka heartbeat.  
**Negative space:** Floral-air and restrained ozonic materials keep the cypress from turning into a pine-cleaner wall.  
**Recurrence:** Cypress returns in the late drydown as a dry green filament through tonka and musk.  
**Texture progression:** mandarin flash → cold needles → aromatic fabric → almond warmth → musky pulse  
**Spatial identity:** Strong cool projection, then a dense warm aura that hugs closer than the EDT.  
**Late residue:** Cypress-dry woods, tonka, soft sandalwood, labdanum and radiant skin musk.  
**Controlled imperfection:** A peppery resinous needle edge prevents the dense warmth from becoming smooth to the point of anonymity.

| Source system | Target system | Effect | Phase | Expected effect | Failure mode | Physical test |

| AROMATIC COOLING | CYPRESS SPINE | enhancement | opening_heart | Lavender, sage and cool terpenes make cypress athletic rather than forest-like. | Menthol, medicine or fougère cliché. | Aromatic block at three levels. |
| COOL AIR | CYPRESS SPINE | diffusion_change | heart | Floral-air materials expand the coniferous signal and prevent density. | Ozonic shower gel. | Air-block omission and distance profile. |
| CYPRESS SPINE | TONKA WARMTH | temperature_change | heart_to_drydown | Green-cold propulsion folds into almond warmth. | Pine play-dough. | Cypress/tonka mixture design. |
| WHITE-MUSK VOLUME | TONKA WARMTH | rounding | drydown | Musks create an enveloping warm field around tonka. | Laundry and sweetness flattening. | Musk block ±20%. |
| CYPRESS SPINE | WHITE-MUSK VOLUME | persistence_change | drydown | A dry cypress filament should persist inside the musk field. | Cypress vanishes; generic intense musk remains. | 8 h cypress recognizer with no-cypress control. |
| WOODY FRAME | CYPRESS SPINE | bridge | heart_to_drydown | Transparent and creamy woods translate needles into polished structure. | Dry wood monolith. | Wood-family ablations. |
| QUIET COLOR | TONKA WARMTH | texture_change | late_heart | Ionone/floral traces keep warmth perfumistic and dimensional. | Cosmetic sweetness. | Quiet-color omission after maturation. |
| MANDARIN SPARKLE | WHITE-MUSK VOLUME | masking | opening | Heavy musk may mute mandarin sparkle. | Muted top and immediate laundry. | Low/high musk with same citrus load. |

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** Full Cypress Pulse versus no-cypress, low/high cypress, low/high tonka, and exact mandarin comparator.

**Ablation blocks**

- MANDARIN SPARKLE
- CYPRESS SPINE
- AROMATIC COOLING
- COOL AIR
- WOODY FRAME
- TONKA WARMTH
- WHITE-MUSK VOLUME

**Perturbation controls**

- cypress ±20%
- aromatic cooling ±20%
- tonka warmth ±20%
- white musk ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- cypress is recognizer through 2 h
- cool-to-warm pulse is legible
- no pine cleaner or menthol gel
- tonka warm without play-dough
- musk strong without laundry flattening
- positive repeated-day liking
- distinct from AHS EDT

**Reference gate:** Verified current bottle, exact mandarin, and botanically verified cypress.

**Temporal question:** Does cypress detail survive the dense tonka-musk envelope?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

**Target identity:** Persistent grapefruit and a multi-temperature spice crown over floral non-camphor lavender, licorice velvet and rich ambery woods.

**Version scope:** Current Elixir architecture.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Red Moon Licorice  
**Primary motif:** A persistent grapefruit spear surrounded by hot cinnamon/nutmeg and cool cardamom.  
**Secondary motif:** A floral lavender crown sinking into sweet licorice velvet and dark polished woods.  
**Tension:** Explosive spice-freshness versus velvety dark smoothness.  
**Release:** The sharp spear is wrapped first in lavender, then in licorice, until only a dark red trace remains in the woods.  
**Negative space:** Floral transmission and distributed woods stop the formula from becoming a compressed spice-amber wall.  
**Recurrence:** Grapefruit bitterness returns late as a dry mineral edge inside licorice, vetiver and amberwood.  
**Texture progression:** bitter spear → hot/cool spice corona → lavender velvet → licorice lacquer → dark wood  
**Spatial identity:** The opening radiates in all directions; the late structure is denser, lower and more controlled.  
**Late residue:** Licorice, patchouli, vetiver, incense, dark oud-suede and distributed amberwood pressure.  
**Controlled imperfection:** A faint phenolic, bitter and smoky edge keeps the licorice from becoming candy.

## C. MISSING-CHEMICAL IMPACT

| Function | Coverage | Candidate | Candidate state | Classification | Why nonredundant | Loss if omitted | Failure mode | Exact comparison |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Nutmeg warmth in multi-temperature spice crown | `MISSING` | Nutmeg EO | `MISSING` | **ESSENTIAL UNBLOCKER** | Cardamom and cinnamon cover coolness and heat but not nutmeg's dry, warm, slightly woody-spicy middle temperature. | The spice crown becomes binary and less rounded. | Culinary potpourri, dusty spice or woody dryness. | Cinnamon-cardamom control versus +nutmeg at 0.5×/1×/1.5× in the grapefruit-lavender chassis. |
| Ceylon-cinnamon heat | `WEAK` | Cinnamomum zeylanicum bark oil / exact Dior-compatible cinnamon comparator | `MISSING EXACT MATERIAL / CASSIA OWNED` | **HIGH_VALUE ARCHITECTURAL EXPANSION** | Owned cassia is chemically and sensorially different and cannot be silently renamed Ceylon cinnamon. | Heat may be harsher, more cinnamaldehydic and less nuanced. | Candle, potpourri, burning spice and irritation risk. | Cassia 1%, Ceylon 1% and no-cinnamon controls at equal cinnamic registration; safety remains separately gated. |
| AOP Nyons lavender identity | `WEAK` | Documented AOP Nyons lavender lot | `MISSING EXACT ORIGIN / FUNCTION OWNED` | **HIGH_VALUE ARCHITECTURAL EXPANSION** | High-altitude lavender provides aromatic function, but cannot establish the non-camphor, floral, vanilla-coumarin facets Dior attributes to the selected Nyons blend. | Lavender can read generic, soapier or more camphoraceous. | Soap, camphor, medicinal dryness or fougère cliché. | Owned high-altitude lavender versus AOP Nyons lot at equal perceived lavender intensity, with cardamom and rich woods fixed. |
| Sweet smooth licorice velvet | `WEAK` | Licorice Accord 10% v0.1 | `PREPARABLE / NOT YET MIXED` | **ESSENTIAL UNBLOCKER** | Anise EO alone is not a complete licorice accord; the function requires anisic, coumarinic, vanillic, immortelle, benzoin and dark-trace integration. | The rich woods lose their velvety dark sweetness and recognizable Elixir shadow. | Cough syrup, anise candy or medicinal sweetness. | No licorice, anise-only and full prepared accord at equal anisic registration in the rich-wood base. |

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** Full Red Moon Licorice versus no-licorice, anise-only, no-dark-shadow, and multi-temperature spice controls.

**Ablation blocks**

- GRAPEFRUIT SPEAR
- SPICE CROWN
- LAVENDER CROWN
- LICORICE VELVET
- RICH WOODS
- AMBERWOOD PRESSURE
- DARK SHADOW

**Perturbation controls**

- grapefruit spear ±20%
- hot spice ±20%
- cool cardamom ±20%
- lavender crown ±20%
- licorice velvet ±20%
- dark shadow ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- grapefruit persistent and bitter
- hot/cool spice differentiated
- lavender floral not camphor
- licorice smooth not cough syrup
- woods rich but internally articulated
- no amberwood wall
- positive repeated-day liking

**Reference gate:** Current Elixir reference plus nutmeg and exact cinnamon/lavender comparators for full fidelity.

**Temporal question:** Does grapefruit-spice relay through lavender to licorice-rich woods without medicinal collapse?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

## Authority boundary

Source admission, package installation, formula authority, inventory mutation, stock authority, bottle authority, physical execution, sensory and analytical authority, measured headspace, strict empirical OAV, safety, procurement, compounding, repository installation, publication and release remain false.

# YSL L’Homme Eau de Toilette

**Formula artifact state:** `PASS_EXACT_DECIMAL_SUCCESSOR`  
**Stock-lineage state:** `HOLD`  
**Successor formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-LHOMME-v2`  
**Successor hash:** `631a1e77edc0d2f8188763dfa95294946a6e47b0595861da3553c53e0e2e4f02`  
**Parent formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-LHOMME-v1`  
**Parent hash:** `2821caa6b573b4b0fc68fadaa4af56f286e073afcbe6ea0d8cc7975102e94d2a`

**Target identity:** Polished citrus-white-pepper sparkle, ginger kinetics and violet-leaf/basil tension rooted in vetiver, tonka and cedar.

**Version scope:** Current L’Homme Eau de Toilette, preserving international architecture.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Urban Ginger Metal  
**Primary motif:** White-pepper light and ginger movement flashing across metallic violet leaf.  
**Secondary motif:** A basil-flower kink and polished vetiver-tonka-cedar root.  
**Tension:** Sharp kinetic freshness versus smooth urban sensuality.  
**Release:** The metallic green heart is polished by floral air and descends into a clean, warm woody root.  
**Negative space:** Hedione and muguet materials create breathing room around ginger and violet leaf, preventing toothpaste/cucumber collision.  
**Recurrence:** The ginger disappears after an hour, then returns as a dry warm shimmer over vetiver and cedar.  
**Texture progression:** pepper light → ginger snap → metallic leaf → floral polish → warm woody skin  
**Spatial identity:** High-definition kinetic near field with a clean polished woody aura at distance.  
**Late residue:** Vetiver, cedar, smooth sandalwood, tonka, incense and clean macrocyclic musk.  
**Controlled imperfection:** An anisic basil kink and a trace metallic leaf bitterness keep the polish from feeling corporate and generic.

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** Full Urban Ginger Metal versus white/black/no-pepper, basil 0/low/high, and ginger/leaf ratio controls.

**Ablation blocks**

- CITRUS-PEPPER FLASH
- GINGER KINETICS
- VIOLET LEAF METAL
- BASIL FLOWER KINK
- FLORAL-SPICE BRIDGE
- WOODY ROOT
- TONKA ROOT

**Perturbation controls**

- pepper ±20%
- ginger ±20%
- violet leaf ±20%
- basil kink ±20%
- woody root ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- pepper reads polished and white-light
- ginger stays kinetic not candy
- violet leaf metallic without cucumber
- basil distinctive without pesto
- root smooth but not hollow
- positive repeated-day liking
- distinct from La Nuit

**Reference gate:** Current international EDT reference plus qualified white-pepper material.

**Temporal question:** Do ginger, violet leaf and basil remain recognizable beyond the first flash?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

## Authority boundary

Source admission, package installation, formula authority, inventory mutation, stock authority, bottle authority, physical execution, sensory and analytical authority, measured headspace, strict empirical OAV, safety, procurement, compounding, repository installation, publication and release remain false.

# YSL La Nuit de L’Homme Eau de Toilette

**Formula artifact state:** `PASS_EXACT_DECIMAL_SUCCESSOR`  
**Stock-lineage state:** `HOLD`  
**Successor formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-LANUIT-v2`  
**Successor hash:** `6ebad0f5788f1035f9a0e242d3dfa044d77b3400b63af562a2810a3433bb498d`  
**Parent formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-LANUIT-v1`  
**Parent hash:** `ace858cca8c70d68e9b7634bb248a7a814bee38e461cb751f2fd5414bebee6d1`

**Target identity:** An intimate cardamom-led night structure with cool aromatic lift, soft floral/powder shadow and cedar-vetiver-tonka/coumarin warmth.

**Version scope:** Current Eau de Toilette with unresolved regional official-source conflict.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Cardamom Velvet  
**Primary motif:** Cardamom warmth glowing inside a dark cedar frame.  
**Secondary motif:** A cool lavender-floral shadow with a low iris whisper over tonka-coumarin skin.  
**Tension:** Restraint versus abandon at close range.  
**Release:** The bright cardamom is slowly absorbed by cedar and tonka until it becomes warm skin rather than a spice note.  
**Negative space:** Hedione, muguet and restrained transparent wood preserve intimacy without making the perfume weak or opaque.  
**Recurrence:** Cardamom returns as a warm pulse at two and eight hours, no longer bright but embedded in coumarin and musk.  
**Texture progression:** electric cardamom → cool aromatic velvet → iris haze → dark cedar → warm skin  
**Spatial identity:** Highly legible within conversational distance, deliberately restrained in the far field.  
**Late residue:** Cedar, tonka-coumarin, vetiver, resin, suede and soft macrocyclic musk.  
**Controlled imperfection:** A faint smoky animalic shadow under the polished warmth creates nocturnal depth without fecal or bandage character.

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** US-emphasis target, international-emphasis target, and Cardamom Velvet art formula, each with 0/low/medium iris shadow.

**Ablation blocks**

- CARDAMOM NIGHT
- BERGAMOT ELECTRIC LIFT
- LAVENDER HEART
- SOFT IRIS SHADOW
- CEDAR DARK FRAME
- TONKA-COUMARIN WARMTH
- INTIMATE MUSK

**Perturbation controls**

- cardamom ±20%
- lavender heart ±20%
- iris shadow 0/0.5×/1×/1.5×
- tonka-coumarin ±20%
- intimate musk ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- cardamom recognizable through 2 h
- near-field detail exceeds far-field loudness
- iris never becomes lipstick or DHI-like
- warmth sensual not syrupy
- cedar dark not generic
- positive repeated-day liking
- source branch selected only against locked bottle

**Reference gate:** Authenticated exact current EDT bottle is an essential unblocker; source conflict remains HOLD.

**Temporal question:** Does close-range cardamom remain attractive as cedar and coumarin/tonka emerge?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

# Bleu de Chanel Parfum

**Formula artifact state:** `PASS_EXACT_DECIMAL_SUCCESSOR`  
**Stock-lineage state:** `HOLD`  
**Successor formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-BDCP-v2`  
**Successor hash:** `a98cee9f56b8201ff4b3cf2a48b19e91c792dfe2da65179b77f37a4ff9ba4128`  
**Parent formula:** `FIN-HEDONIC-BDCP-v1`  
**Parent hash:** `cd77515d72f4cfe28ada73622c3b80a019947403269bbcb39597e197d1f141dd`

**Target identity:** A seamless sweep from bitter-aromatic freshness through full cedar into creamy New Caledonian sandalwood and polished musky woods.

**Version scope:** Current Parfum architecture.

## Artistic architecture preserved

**Art title:** Blue Cedar Continuum  
**Primary motif:** Bitter aromatic freshness that travels through full cedar instead of disappearing above it.  
**Secondary motif:** New Caledonian-style sandalwood cream, incense air and dark musky wood.  
**Tension:** Invigorating clarity versus dense creamy authority.  
**Release:** Freshness is translated into aromatic wood, then into creamy sandalwood, so the perfume feels continuous rather than top-heavy.  
**Negative space:** Incense air, floral transmission and dark shadow create depth between the cedar and sandalwood masses.  
**Recurrence:** Grapefruit bitterness reappears as a dry mineral edge around the late sandalwood.  
**Texture progression:** bitter blue light → aromatic air → cedar grain → sandalwood cream → dark polished aura  
**Spatial identity:** Clear bitter freshness projects first; the far field becomes polished wood while the near field retains incense and cream.  
**Late residue:** Cedar, sandalwood, incense, mineral amber, musk, moss and a restrained suede-oud shadow.  
**Controlled imperfection:** A trace of smoke, moss and root darkness prevents the smooth woods from becoming a showroom surface.

| New Caledonian sandalwood identity | `MISSING` | Documented New Caledonian sandalwood lot | `MISSING` | **HIGH_VALUE ARCHITECTURAL EXPANSION** | Current synthetic and unspecified sandalwood stocks cover cream, radiance and persistence but not the official origin-specific natural identity. | The Parfum may be deep and smooth but less specifically Chanel-like in its warm wood signature. | Creamy monolith, buttery wood or loss of cedar contrast. | Current distributed sandalwood architecture versus exact natural lot, while cedar mass and total wood load remain fixed. |
| Continuous freshness-through-wood sweep | `EVIDENCE_GAP` | Dynamic headspace plus coded temporal sensory data | `NOT TESTED` | **EVIDENCE GAP, NOT INVENTORY GAP** | Static formula composition cannot prove that freshness appears to linger while cedar and sandalwood become dominant. | The fragrance may split into citrus top and heavy wood base rather than one continuous sweep. | Woody monolith, generic blue cloud or pencil-cedar dryness. | Full build versus bridge-block ablation and −20% wood-load controls; timepoints 0, 5, 30 min, 2 h, 8 h and 24 h. |

### Candidate decisions

- **Documented New Caledonian sandalwood lot** — **HIGH_VALUE ARCHITECTURAL EXPANSION** — High-value target-fidelity acquisition, not an automatic formula addition.
- **Dynamic headspace plus coded temporal sensory data** — **EVIDENCE GAP, NOT INVENTORY GAP** — Run evidence program before buying more woody materials.

**Ambrettolide 10% in DPG:** `PLANNED ACQUISITION / DESIGN-AVAILABLE`; procurement and ExactStockRef remain pending. No physical addition is claimed.

## E. CONTROLLED TEST PLAN

**Primary comparison:** Full Blue Cedar Continuum versus no-freshness bridge, −20% cedar, −20% sandalwood, and exact New Caledonian sandalwood comparator.

**Ablation blocks**

- BITTER AROMATIC FRESHNESS
- AROMATIC RELAY
- FLORAL AIR
- CEDAR BODY
- SANDALWOOD CREAM
- DARK NEGATIVE SPACE
- AMBER SKELETON
- MUSKY WOOD AURA

**Perturbation controls**

- aromatic relay ±20%
- floral-air bridge ±20%
- cedar body ±20%
- sandalwood cream ±20%
- dark negative space ±20%

**Pass criteria**

- freshness continues through 2 h
- cedar and sandalwood remain distinct yet continuous
- no pencil or buttery monolith
- dark space adds depth without smoke dominance
- late residue recognizable and polished
- positive repeated-day liking
- distinct from AHS

**Reference gate:** Current Parfum reference plus exact New Caledonian sandalwood comparator for full fidelity.

**Temporal question:** Does invigorating freshness seem to sweep continuously through cedar and sandalwood?

- Early sampling: 0, 5, 15, 30 min
- Late sampling: 2 h, 8 h, 24 h
- Replication: Two non-consecutive days; matched application geometry
- Sensory lane: Coded descriptive time-intensity plus hard-defect checklist
- Instrumental lane: HS-SPME-GC-MS at 0/30 min/2 h/8 h on sealed vial and blotter; static and dynamic data remain separate
- Thermodynamic inputs: Exact stock amounts, ethanol/water matrix, temperature, substrate, dose area, vapor pressure, activity-coefficient model/version

Source admission, package installation, formula authority, inventory mutation, stock authority, bottle authority, physical execution, sensory and analytical authority, measured headspace, strict empirical OAV, safety, procurement, compounding, repository installation, publication and release remain false.
